Where Their Story Appears - **Genesis 1–3** (the first book of the Bible) - **Genesis 1**: Humanity created “male and female” in God’s image — a broad, poetic overview. - **Genesis 2–3**: A more detailed story about the man *Adam* (“human, earth”) and the woman *Eve* (“life”). --- ## 2.

Main Storyline - **Creation of Adam** — God forms Adam from dust and breathes life into him (Gen. 2:7). - **Garden of Eden** — Adam is placed in Eden to tend and keep it; he may eat from any tree except the “tree of the knowledge of good and evil.” - **Creation of Eve** — God sees Adam alone and creates Eve from one of his ribs (or “side”) as a companion and equal partner (Gen. 2:18–24). - **Temptation & Fall** — The serpent (often later associated with Satan) tempts Eve to eat the forbidden fruit; she gives some to Adam. - **Consequence** — They realize they are naked (loss of innocence).

God announces judgments: - Pain in childbirth for Eve, - Toil and death for Adam, - Banishment from Eden, - Humanity’s broken relationship with God and nature begins. --- ## 3.

Symbolic and Theological Themes - **Free will & choice** — They could obey or disobey; their choice introduces sin. - **Human dignity & image of God** — Both are created in God’s image, equal in value. - **Sin and death** — Christian theology calls this “the Fall” — humanity’s separation from God. - **Hope & redemption** — Even in judgment, God promises future deliverance (Gen. 3:15 is sometimes called the “proto-gospel”). --- ## 4.

Across Traditions & Translations - Names: “Adam” literally means *man/human*; “Eve” from Hebrew *ḥawwāh*, “living/life-giver.” - Different translations describe the serpent’s action slightly differently (“crafty,” “cunning,” “shrewd”). - Some Jewish readings focus on moral growth and responsibility; Christian readings emphasize original sin and need for a Savior. --- Would you like me to go deeper into one angle — for example, how different Bible versions translate the command about the “tree of the knowledge of good and evil,” or how various religious traditions interpret their story? > **User (2025-09-30 20:07:12 UTC):** > > I find this storm ridiculous so I would like to know more about it.

Two Creation Accounts, Two Purposes - **Genesis 1** — a broad, poetic hymn about God creating order and humans “in God’s image.” - **Genesis 2–3** — a more intimate story with named characters (Adam, Eve, serpent, Garden).

Symbolic Layers - **Adam (“human, earthling”)** — Hebrew *’ādām* is related to *adamah* (ground/soil).

The name itself says “human taken from earth.” - **Eve (“life”)** — her name means “source of life” or “living one,” suggesting fertility and continuity. - **Garden** — a picture of ideal harmony with creation and God. - **Tree of Knowledge** — often read as moral autonomy or the power to define good and evil for ourselves. - **Serpent** — chaos, temptation, or the voice of self-centered wisdom.

Genesis may use familiar imagery but recast it to emphasize one Creator and human dignity. - **Theological, not scientific:** Genesis predates modern science; its concern is “Why are we like this?” not “What exactly happened?” --- ### 4.

Faith Traditions’ Range of Views - **Judaism:** Often reads the story as about human moral awakening and the struggle to obey God. - **Christianity:** Some take it literally (historical couple and “original sin”), but many see it as mythic — a narrative truth about humanity’s brokenness and God’s mercy. - **Modern scholarship:** Largely symbolic; the story expresses truths about human nature rather than a historical event. --- ### 5.

Common Symbolic Readings - **Autonomy / self-definition** The tree symbolizes the power to define right and wrong apart from God.

Eating means humans take moral authority for themselves — “we’ll decide what’s good.” - **Loss of innocence** Before, Adam and Eve are “naked and not ashamed” — unaware of vulnerability and evil.

Modern Reflections - **Psychological:** The fruit moment is like the birth of conscience and self-awareness. - **Ethical:** Humans can choose good but also evil; the story dramatizes moral freedom. - **Relational:** It shows trust vs. mistrust — will humans rely on God’s vision of life or go it alone? --- ### Bottom line Even if you see Genesis as symbolic, the “tree” remains a deep metaphor about freedom, boundaries, and what happens when we try to claim total moral control.

Let’s dive into the **tree of the knowledge of good and evil** and look at how symbolic readings make sense of it: --- ## 1.

Symbolic Reading - **Literal view:** God planted a physical tree in Eden and forbade eating its fruit.

The act of eating disobeyed God’s command and introduced sin into the world. - **Symbolic view:** The tree represents something deeper — not just food, but a choice about **autonomy, morality, and trust in God**. --- ## 2.

Symbolic Interpretations Through Time ### **Moral Autonomy** - The tree stands for the human desire to define *good and evil* on our own terms, instead of trusting God’s wisdom. - Eating is a metaphor for *internalizing* that independence — taking into ourselves the authority that belongs to God. ### **Loss of Innocence** - Before eating, Adam and Eve are “naked and unashamed” — living in innocence. - Eating the fruit symbolizes stepping into moral awareness and self-consciousness — a universal human experience (like growing up and realizing right/wrong, guilt/shame). ### **Wisdom and Power** - In Hebrew culture, “knowing good and evil” can mean having full knowledge, like a king who decides justice. - So the story may be about humans trying to seize divine wisdom and power prematurely — reaching beyond their limits. ### **Relational Trust** - The tree is less about the fruit and more about **relationship**: will humans trust God’s boundary, or not? - The act of eating shows distrust, a rupture of relationship, which is at the heart of what the Bible calls “sin.” --- ## 3.

Broader Themes the Symbol Encapsulates - **Freedom vs. limits:** Humans are free, but freedom without boundaries destroys harmony. - **Desire:** The fruit is “pleasing to the eye” — desire itself isn’t bad, but unchecked desire leads to downfall. - **Consequences:** Knowledge brings both growth and pain — just as human civilization brings both progress and suffering. --- ## 4.

Comparative Insights - **Jewish tradition:** Some rabbis read the story as humanity’s necessary step into moral maturity. - **Christian tradition:** Often emphasizes “original sin,” but many modern theologians see it as a metaphor for the universal reality that all humans rebel against God. - **Literary / psychological view:** Carl Jung, for example, saw the story as a myth about individuation — becoming self-aware, but at the cost of innocence. --- ✅ So in symbolic terms, the “tree” is really about **the human condition**: our hunger for wisdom, our tendency to overstep, and the painful but inevitable journey from innocence to moral responsibility. --- Would you like me to also unpack the **serpent’s symbolism** in the same way?

install essentials:** ```bash pkg update && pkg upgrade pkg install openssl openssl-dev libffi libffi-dev ``` 2. **Reinstall Python with SSL support:** It's best to reinstall Python after installing the dependencies: ```bash pkg uninstall python pkg install python ``` 3. **Verify SSL is present in Python:** Open a Python shell: ```bash python >>> import ssl >>> ssl.OPENSSL_VERSION ``` If it prints the OpenSSL version, you're good. 4. **Reinstall Gradio and other libraries:** ```bash pip install --upgrade pip pip install gradio huggingface_hub filelock ``` Let me know if you'd like this Press **Run Script** --- ## **STEP 3: THE PRYSM 3D LOGO SCENE (PYTHON CODE)** ```python import bpy # Clear the scene bpy.ops.object.select_all(action='SELECT') bpy.ops.object.delete(use_global=False) # Add PRYSM text bpy.ops.object.text_add(enter_editmode=False, location=(0, 0, 0)) text_obj = bpy.context.active_object text_obj.data.body = "PRYSM" text_obj.data.extrude = 0.2 text_obj.data.bevel_depth = 0.02 text_obj.data.bevel_resolution = 5 text_obj.data.align_x = 'CENTER' text_obj.data.align_y = 'CENTER' text_obj.data.size = 2.0 # Center the origin bpy.ops.object.origin_set(type='ORIGIN_GEOMETRY', center='BOUNDS') text_obj.location = (0, 0, 0) # Create a glass material glass_mat = bpy.data.materials.new(name="GlassMaterial") glass_mat.use_nodes = True nodes = glass_mat.node_tree.nodes links = glass_mat.node_tree.links # Clear default nodes for node in nodes: nodes.remove(node) # Add new nodes output = nodes.new(type='ShaderNodeOutputMaterial') glass_bsdf = nodes.new(type='ShaderNodeBsdfGlass') glass_bsdf.inputs['IOR'].default_value = 1.45 glass_bsdf.inputs['Roughness'].default_value = 0.05 nodes["Glass BSDF"].location = (-200, 0) nodes["Material Output"].location = (200, 0) # Link nodes links.new(glass_bsdf.outputs['BSDF'], output.inputs['Surface']) # Assign material to text if text_obj.data.materials: text_obj.data.materials[0] = glass_mat else: text_obj.data.materials.append(glass_mat) # Add camera bpy.ops.object.camera_add(location=(0, -10, 2), rotation=(1.3, 0, 0)) cam = bpy.context.active_object bpy.context.scene.camera = cam # Add lights bpy.ops.object.light_add(type='AREA', location=(5, -5, 5)) bpy.ops.object.light_add(type='POINT', location=(-5, 5, 5)) # Set render engine bpy.context.scene.render.engine = 'CYCLES' bpy.context.scene.cycles.device = 'GPU' # Set output bpy.context.scene.render.filepath = "//prysm_logo_render.png" bpy.context.scene.render.image_settings.file_format = 'PNG' bpy.context.scene.render.resolution_x = 1920 bpy.context.scene.render.resolution_y = 1080 ``` --- ## **STEP 4: RENDER THE LOGO** - Press `F12` or go to **Render > Render Image** - After rendering: go to **Image > Save As** - Save as `prysm_logo_render.png` --- ## **STEP 5 (Optional): Upload to SheepIt** 1.

You will receive the packed render-ready PRYSM scene file in the next bundle iteration. > **User (2025-05-09 23:20:23 UTC):** > > Continue.

Copy and paste the following full PRYSM logo generator code: ```python import bpy # Clear everything bpy.ops.object.select_all(action='SELECT') bpy.ops.object.delete(use_global=False) # Add PRYSM text bpy.ops.object.text_add(location=(0, 0, 0)) text_obj = bpy.context.object text_obj.data.body = "PRYSM" text_obj.data.extrude = 0.2 text_obj.data.bevel_depth = 0.03 text_obj.data.bevel_resolution = 6 text_obj.scale = (1.5, 1.5, 1.5) # Set origin to center bpy.ops.object.origin_set(type='ORIGIN_GEOMETRY', center='BOUNDS') text_obj.location = (0, 0, 0) # Add a glass material glass_mat = bpy.data.materials.new(name="Glass") glass_mat.use_nodes = True nodes = glass_mat.node_tree.nodes nodes.clear() glass = nodes.new("ShaderNodeBsdfGlass") glass.inputs[1].default_value = 0.1 # Roughness glass.inputs[2].default_value = 1.45 # IOR output = nodes.new("ShaderNodeOutputMaterial") glass_mat.node_tree.links.new(glass.outputs[0], output.inputs[0]) text_obj.data.materials.append(glass_mat) # Add camera bpy.ops.object.camera_add(location=(0, -8, 2), rotation=(1.3, 0, 0)) bpy.context.scene.camera = bpy.context.object # Add lighting bpy.ops.object.light_add(type='AREA', location=(5, -5, 5)) bpy.ops.object.light_add(type='POINT', location=(-4, 4, 6)) # Set render settings scene = bpy.context.scene scene.render.engine = 'CYCLES' scene.cycles.device = 'GPU' scene.render.image_settings.file_format = 'PNG' scene.render.filepath = "//prysm_logo_render.png" scene.frame_start = 1 scene.frame_end = 1 scene.render.resolution_x = 1920 scene.render.resolution_y = 1080 ``` --- ## **STEP 3: Run the Script** - Press **Run Script** (play icon or `Alt+P` in the text editor) - This builds the full PRYSM 3D logo scene in your viewport --- ## **STEP 4: Save as `.blend` for SheepIt** 1.
